**559050 AP 2-D Art and Design, 1 academic credit                                      Grade(s): 9-12** 

Recommended Prerequisite(s): Pre-AP Visual Arts, Visual Art Foundations II, or Portfolio Submission 

Introductory college-level two-dimensional design course. Refine and apply 2-D skills to ideas developed throughout the course. This is a College Board Advanced Placement course. 

Notes:  Students must submit the AP Portfolio in May in order to receive the weighted GPA credit.  See General Information on Advanced Placement. 

Fee:  $15.00 materials fee. 

**559061       AP 3-D Art and Design, 1 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** 

Recommended Prerequisite(s): Pre-AP Visual Arts, Visual Art Foundations II, or Portfolio Submission 

Introductory college-level three-dimensional design course. Refine and apply 3-D skills to ideas developed throughout the course. This is a College Board Advanced Placement course. 

Notes:  Students must submit the AP Portfolio in May in order to receive weighted GPA credit.  See General Information on Advanced Placement. 

Fee:  $15.00 materials fee. 

**559820       IB Visual Arts SL, 1 academic credit Grade(s): 11-12** 

Prerequisite(s): Pre-AP Visual Arts, Visual Art Foundations II, or Portfolio Submission 

Challenge one's own creative and cultural expectations and boundaries. Develop analytical skills in problem-solving and divergent thinking, while working towards technical proficiency and confidence as art-makers. Explore and compare visual arts from different perspectives and in different contexts. Engage in, experiment with, and critically reflect upon a wide range of contemporary practices and media. 

Fee:  $15.00 materials fee. 

Notes: BHS campus only.  Students who are not pursuing the IB Diploma but complete both years of IB English HL and an additional IB course will have any fees charged by the IB Organization waived. See General Information on International Baccalaureate (IB) Diploma Programme. 

###### **459150 Pre-AP Theatre, 1 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** 

###### Prerequisite(s):  None 

Focus on observation, investigation, and discussion of anchor works of art central to themes and content. Engage in structured conversations, critique work, discuss next steps of reflective writing throughout the process, and creative experimentation of developing work. 

Notes: Attending theatre department productions is a requirement of this course. Standard play ticket price for students is $5.00; standard musical ticket price for students is $10. Student ushers receive free admission. 

**459100       Theatre I, 1 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** 

###### Prerequisite(s): None 

Master both academic and performance skills in the art of drama and theatrical production through improvisation, expressive movement, analysis of scripts and characters, acting, and stagecraft. Evaluate and critique both written and performed works and make connections between theatre, other art forms, and other cultures. 

Notes:  Attending theatre department productions is a requirement of this course.  Standard play ticket price for students is $5.00; standard musical ticket price for students is $10.  Student ushers receive free admission.

81 

**451102 Philharmonic Orchestra, 1 academic credit (451112, 451122, 451132)  BHS only 451108 Symphonic/Sinfonia Orchestra, 1 academic credit (451118, 451128, 451138)** 

Demonstrate an ability to apply music fundamentals and instrumental techniques in the production, performance, analysis, and critique of instrumental music performance. Apply sight-reading skills, improvisational skills, and performance techniques in solo, small group, and large group settings. Critique music performances and deeply reflect upon the impact of instrumental music on society, as well as societal influences on instrumental music. Perform in a variety of settings and will demonstrate successful completion of student learning expectations. 

Students who are in Chamber Orchestra, Symphonic Orchestra, Wind Ensemble, Symphonic Winds, or in 9<sup>th</sup> Grade are required to participate in All-Region and Clinic (if selected) as a part of the course requirement. Students must also participate in All-State and Clinic (if selected) as a part of the course requirement. 

Auditions: Students must audition each year to maintain their membership in a musical ensemble. Students will not register for a particular group (Chamber, Concert, Jazz, etc.). Instead, students will be able to register for a placeholder and will be placed in the appropriate ensemble based on rosters provided by the director(s). After spring auditions, student course requests will be updated automatically to include a primary course request for the appropriate ensemble. 

Instrumental Expenses: Various fees apply. Costs could include instruments/instrument rentals, travel, and lodging for All-Region/All-State and other regional competitions. 

Marching Band Co-Curricular Activity: The default band enrollment is that students perform with the band in all football games, basketball games, pep rallies, parades, concerts, festivals, and marching competitions. Rehearsals begin in July prior to the start of the school year and continue in after-school sessions through the year. Students must be willing to spend this extra time to be successful members of the band program.  Marching Band entails related fees, required practices after school, and required practices in July and August. 

Marching Band Exemption: Participation in Marching Band is not required for Academic/Concert Band program participation. Students may select Band participation as an academic course during regular school day instruction only. Students in the academic band only option will not have band camp, football, marching, or other after school marching activities.  These students could have occasional after-school section rehearsals and will have evening concerts year round and concert band competitions in the spring semester.  Schedule for a Marching Band Exemption with counselors in the Student Success Planning process or related, course-request conversations, by spring break each year. Declarations to exempt Marching Band must be made before the June 1 schedule verification deadline at the latest and do not qualify for schedule change options in the fall without approval from the principal or Director of VPA. 

PE Credit for Marching Band: Students involved in marching band outside the school hours will receive PE credit for that physical activity. 

**Vocal Ensembles: 452005 Concert Choir, 1 academic credit (452045, 452055, 452065) Grade(s) 9-12 45200M Choir Advanced 9 – Tenor-Bass, 1 academic credit Grade(s): 9 45200W Choir Advanced 9 - Treble, 1 academic credit                               Grade(s): 9 452043 Sophomore Select, 1 academic credit                                             Grade(s): 10 452041 Bella Voce, 1 academic credit (452051, 452061)   BHS only Grade(s): 10-12 45205A A Cappella, 1 academic credit (45206A)                                         Grade(s):11-12 45205C Chamber Choir, 1 academic credit (45206C)                                 Grade(s):11-12**

High School Course Catalog, January 2026 

82 

Demonstrate an ability to apply music fundamentals and vocal techniques in the production, performance, analysis, and critique of vocal music performance. Apply sight-reading skills, improvisational skills, and performance techniques in solo, small group, and large group settings. Critique vocal music performances and deeply reflect upon the impact of vocal music on society, as well as societal influences on vocal music. 

Vocal music students can develop competency in a variety of Specialized Vocal Ensemble courses, including but not limited to Concert Choir, 9th (Advanced/On-level) Treble, and  9th (Advanced/On-level) Tenor-Bass. In these ensemble courses, the major weight and work of the discipline falls in the performing process. Although the artistic processes of creating, responding, and connecting are important, the concepts covered in these processes are accessible only after students are thoroughly versed in the fundamental skills necessary to perform in an ensemble. 

Auditions: Students wishing to perform in advanced ensembles are required to audition with a director in the spring for the following year. Students are able to register for “Choir” as a placeholder and will be placed in the appropriate choir (advanced/on-level) based on rosters provided by choir directors. After spring auditions, student course requests will be updated automatically to include a primary course request for the appropriate choir. Students in a Vocal Ensemble course will participate in several concerts and/or contests during the school year. Extra rehearsals may be required. Each student will be responsible for the required performance attire. Each student has the opportunity to audition for the All-Region Choir (Advanced, required; On-level, strongly encouraged). 

Fees: Various fees apply. Costs could include travel and lodging for All-Region/All-State and other regional competitions. 

###### **451500       Music Lab I (Guitar), 1 academic credit                                          Grade(s): 9-12** Prerequisite(s): None 

Develop beginner-level guitar skills such as academic vocabulary of music, music notation, and instrumental techniques. Explore a brief history of the guitar along with a study of its respective musical styles. Learn to read music notation, chord symbols, and tablature. Understand different musical genres, including classical, flamenco, blues, jazz, rock, and pop music. 

**459010       Music Theory, 1 academic credit                                                      Grade(s): 10-12** Prerequisite(s):  Band I, Orchestra I, Jazz Band I, or Vocal Ensemble I 

Expand and enhance the skills of the serious high school musicians by examining components of music composition, melodic practices, theories of harmony, and other musical concepts. Analyze music from different stylistic periods and develop notation, aural, and sight-reading skills. Emphasis is placed on the application of rhythm, melody, harmony, form, and other compositional devices into original compositions. 

###### **559011       AP Music Theory, 1 academic credit                                                Grade(s): 9-12** Prerequisite(s): None 

Introductory college-level music theory course. Cultivate understanding of music theory through analyzing performed and notated music. Explore concepts of pitch, rhythm, form, and musical design. This is a College Board Advanced Placement course. 

Notes: Students must take this course’s AP exam in May in order to receive weighted credit. See General Information on Advanced Placement.

###### **999880 PSAT/SAT/ACT Test Prep, .5 Non-Academic Credit Grade(s): 9-12** 

###### Prerequisite(s): None 

PSAT/SAT/ACT Test Prep prepares students for the PSAT, SAT, and ACT. It gives students the opportunity to practice the skills they need to succeed on standardized exams, as well as gain the knowledge and critical thinking skills that will aid them in all classes and prepare them for life or schooling beyond high school. This course is a non-academic credit and is not calculated into the student’s GPA. 

###### **496010 Community Service Learning, 1 Academic Credit** 

###### **Grade(s): 9-12** 

###### Prerequisite(s): Application 

Act 648 of 1993 states that beginning with the 1996-97 school year, "a student who has completed a minimum of 75 clock hours of documented community service in grades nine through 12, shall be eligible to receive one academic credit that may be applied toward graduation."  Act 237 of 2023 converts this program to a requirement for students in the Class of 2027 and beyond. See details at Bentonville Schools > Menu > Departments > Academics > Service Learning. This is not a class in the school day. This is a credit earned outside of the school day. Service should be vetted and planned with a parent.  Students maintain a log of all service.  Signatures or forms are not required from every agency served. Campus administrators will collect logs for review at least annually. Credit is awarded in our Triand records in .25 increments for grade levels with this requirement. 

###### **999830 Student Aide, 0 Academic Credit** Prerequisite(s): Application 

###### **Grade(s): 11-12** 

During their junior or senior year, students may have one period scheduled as an aide worker. Aides are assigned to various locations during the school day. This is a class with no credit. This should not be a planned class in any Student Success Plan.

## **Health & PE Course Descriptions** 

PE courses are only counted as an academic credit the first time the course/athletic period is completed. Thereafter the course is a non-academic credit. See more in General Information-Grade Point Average. 

###### **480000       Health, .5 academic credit                                                               Grade(s): 9-12** 

###### **48000V Online** 

Health students will be involved in studying the various aspects of health. Topics include: nutrition, physical fitness, mental health, tobacco and alcohol education, and human sexuality. Lessons will be taught using lecture methods, individual and group work, guest speakers, and demonstrations. Students will receive training and awareness of CPR, first aid, and Stop the Bleed. CPR certification is optional for a fee. 

###### **580900  Wellness Concepts NWACC HLSC 1002, 1 acad. Credit** 

**Grade(s): 9-12** 

Prerequisite(s): See General Information on Concurrent Enrollment and Online Courses and their website for entrance test score requirements. 

Wellness Concepts is an entry-level 16-week Distance Learning course. Students will examine their own personal behaviors in regards to physical activity, nutrition, and other health behaviors with a goal of being able to make changes to create a healthier self. Course objectives include examining current nutritional habits vs actual 

nutritional needs and completing self-assessments designed to see what you need to do to improve your fitness level. In addition, you will create a wellness plan for yourself. The course consists of weekly quizzes with assignments or discussions with fellow students. Upon completion of this course, students will have the knowledge needed to become a healthier individual. 

Notes: See General Information on Concurrent online courses 

**4850210 Physical Education, .5 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** 

Physical Education is designed to promote physical fitness for life. Students will study units through the year built around health-related fitness, health and wellness, and lifetime activities. All students will participate in health-related fitness assessments and will design a personal fitness plan. 

**485020 Rec Sports: Life Fitness & Conditioning, .5 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** Lifetime Fitness & Conditioning is designed to provide students with the knowledge to safely use a weight room; develop the skills to properly perform specific exercises; and to assess, plan, and monitor a personalized fitness program. 

**485020A Rec Sports: Aerobics, .5 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** 

Aerobics combines step aerobics, kick-boxing, yoga, Pilates, and circuit training to increase fitness levels of participants. Approximately 60 minutes per class will be devoted to participation, with the remaining 30 minutes to warm-up, cool-down, and daily goals. Students are required to dress out and participate in class activities. 

**4850130 Outdoor Pursuits PE, .5 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** Outdoor Pursuits PE is a practical course for learning and practicing skills for outdoor activities. Students will have the opportunity to venture out of the traditional classroom through planned field trips incorporating sport. Fee: $30.00 activity fee. 

###### **48504X Athletic Weight Training for GTSB, .5 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** 

This is a course option for student athletes on the roster for the sports of: Golf, Tennis, Swim & Dive, and Bowling. Students are encouraged to be enrolled for both semesters of the school year. The program will focus on general physical preparedness and development of fundamental movement skills to prepare individuals for the competitive seasons. When in-season the program will focus more on sport-specific preparation. Those enrolled in the course will also attend their official practice after school. 

**485950 Navy JROTC PE, .5 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** Navy JROTC can be a PE credit, health credit, or practical arts credit. Students in this course can achieve their CPR awareness requirement. See the full course description in the Career Studies section of this catalog. 



Bentonville Schools 

High School Course Catalog, January 2026 

86 

**480950       Navy JROTC Health, .5 academic credit Grade(s): 9-12** Navy JROTC can be a PE credit, health credit, or practical arts credit. Students in this course can achieve their CPR awareness requirement.See the full course description in the Career Studies section of this catalog. 

**<mark>45114X Marching Band Grade(s): 9 - 12</mark>** Marching Band can be a PE credit, or VPA credit. See the full course description in the Visual & Performing Arts section of this catalog. 

The district is slowly developing more PE options for students who are involved in significant endeavors outside of school. See your counselor for more information.

## **Athletics** 

Students may not register for athletics except to register for early and regular start if listed on the Health & PE Courses At-a-Glance page. If yours is not listed below it will be scheduled by the coach and administration. Following spring/fall tryouts, student course requests will be automatically updated to include a primary request for all other sports based on rosters submitted by the coaches. The purpose for not registering in athletics is to ensure that seats are available for the student in other electives, should the student be unable to participate on the team for any reason. In preparation for making the team, however, you should register for the appropriate start time for the athletic team on which you plan to participate. 

Students in Athletics will work on fundamental skills and compete in interscholastic competitive activities. Participants will be required to attend practices after school and games throughout the year. Athletics teams compete for conference championships in the Northwest Arkansas Conference. The .5 semester credit of PE required for graduation purposes may be satisfied by participating in Athletics. A maximum of one PE credit may be applied toward graduation. 

PE courses are only counted as an “academic credit” the first time the course/athletic period is completed. Thereafter the course is a “non-academic credit”. See more the General Information -“non-academic credits”. 

Students involved in athletic programs and marching band as a “9<sup>th</sup> period” outside the school day such as golf, tennis, swim, bowling, etc. do not receive PE credit for that physical activity in part because students are generally limited to seven credits maximum a time. However, we provide an online module free of charge. Upon completion of the online modules that complement their activity the HPE standards are met and credit is awarded. 

Note: See the start time of each sport and register for the proper start time.

## **Appendix Learn about Yourself** 

Arkansas law requires that the student success plan shall (1) guide the student along the pathway to graduation, (2) address accelerated learning opportunities, (3) address academic deficits and interventions, and (4) include college and career planning components based on student interests. 

To make wise choices, you also will need to understand yourself and your goals for the future. It is important, therefore, to take time to learn more about yourself. 

###### **My Abilities** 

- In which subjects do I do well in school? 

- What do I do well outside of school? 

- Which talents do I have? (Play a musical instrument, sing, paint, dance, act, write, etc.) 

- Which sport(s) do I play well? 

- Will I meet NCAA guidelines for an athletic scholarship? 

###### **My Interests** 

- Which subjects are most interesting to me in school? 

- Which activities are most interesting to me in school? 

- Which activities are most interesting to me outside of school? 

- What are my hobbies? 

###### **My Attitudes** 

- What is important to me in my life? 

- Which people are important to me? 

- Which activities are important to me? 

- Which possessions are important to me? 

###### **My Likes** 

- Which subjects do I like in school? 

- Which activities do I like in school? 

- Which activities do I like outside of school? 

- Do I like to be with other people much of the time? 

- Do I like to be alone much of the time? 

- Do I enjoy working in a group? 

- Do I enjoy working with my hands? 

- Do I enjoy reading? 

- Do I enjoy figuring out how things work? 

- Do I like to use technology & learn new applications? 

###### **My Goals** 

- What do I want to accomplish in high school? 

- What might I want to do after high school? 

## **Freshman Transition** 

The decisions you make now as you consider or revise a four-year graduation plan as well as actions you take throughout high school will greatly affect your future. To increase your potential for fulfilling a successful and prosperous future, you are encouraged to: 

   - Enroll in one or more advanced courses. 

   - Enroll in courses that support your strengths. 

   - Enroll in courses that address your deficits. 

- Choose a series of electives that are related and culminate in internships and/or certifications. 

- Commit to a series of courses or club activities that show commitment and perseverance. 



Bentonville Schools 

High School Course Catalog, January 2026 

91 

## **Aptitude Testing Overview** 

A variety of tests are used to help you as well as your future employers, colleges, universities, and the military consider your aptitude. 

###### **<u>Preliminary SAT/National Merit Scholarship Qualifying Test PSAT/NMSQT</u>** 

The PSAT/NMSQT measures verbal and mathematical reasoning abilities. It serves three purposes: 

- Allows students to compare their academic abilities with other college-bound students at their specific grade level. 

- Familiarizes students with the SAT. 

- Allows college-bound juniors to compete for National Merit Scholarship Recognition. 

The test is offered only in October and should be taken by all college-bound juniors. Sophomores will take the test for practice. 

###### **<u>College Admission Tests</u>** 

Most colleges require ACT or SAT scores to qualify for admission. Registration for each of these tests is done online. It is your responsibility to have your scores sent directly to the colleges of your choice from the testing agency. Junior and Senior students on free or reduced lunch may qualify for PSAT, SAT and/or ACT fee waivers. Check in the College & Career Center to see if you are eligible. Research the test policy and application requirements for schools of interest. 

###### **<u>SAT</u>** 

The SAT is given seven times a year. Testing dates are found on the College Board website or in the College & Career Center at collegeboard.org > <u>SAT</u> 

###### **<u>SAT Subject Tests</u>** 

Highly selective colleges and universities may require the scores of two or more SAT Subject Tests as part of the admissions process. These tests are one-hour long that measure the student’s knowledge of a particular subject area. 

###### **<u>ACT</u>** 

The ACT covers four subject areas: English, Mathematics, Reading, and Science Reasoning. The ACT is offered six times a year. Testing dates and registration can be found on the ACT website at act.org > <u>The ACT Test</u> or in the Student Life Center or College & Career Center. 

###### **<u>ASVAB</u>** 

The Armed Services Vocational Aptitude Battery (ASVAB) is a multiple-aptitude battery that measures developed abilities and helps predict future academic and occupational success in the military. ASVAB is offered on campus. 



###### **<u>ACT WorkKeys National Career Readiness Certificate (NCRC)</u>** 

ACT WorkKeys National Career Readiness Certificate (NCRC) measures and certifies the essential work skills needed for success in jobs across industries and occupations.  Certificates are issued at four levels; Platinum, Gold, Silver, and Bronze.  Some employers require this measure. Arkansas Act 319 of 2021 requires schools to administer this test.  It is a certificate students can use to document their skills for job fairs and interviews. 

###### **<u>Industry Certifications for Careers</u>** 

Bentonville Schools proudly prepares students for industry recognized certification tests in a variety of careers. See the Career Pathway tables for details of certifications for each pathway.

High School Course Catalog, January 2026

## **College Planning Activities for Freshman** 

- **Map out your four-year graduation plan.** All four years should include the core academic subjects (English, math, science, and social studies). Choose the most rigorous courses available to strengthen your college admission opportunities. 

- **Include world language study.** Plan for at least 2–3 years of a world language if you’re considering highly selective schools. 

- Research college entrance requirements **.** Look up admission requirements for colleges that interest you and align your high school program of study accordingly. 

- **Build strong study habits.** These will set you up for success throughout high school and beyond. 

- Plan ahead for IB. If you hope to pursue the IB Diploma Programme, start taking Spanish, French, or Chinese now (if you haven’t already). 

- Get involved. Participate in a variety of extracurricular activities to explore your interests. 

- Volunteer locally. Begin community service in Bentonville and keep a record of your hours. 

- **Make college visits.** As a freshman, you may take one excused day for a college visit. You can also take advantage of summer, holiday breaks, and family vacations to explore campuses. 

- Plan to take the ACT and/or SAT up to three times before fall of senior year. 

- Use Naviance. Log in through Bentonvillek12.org > Schools > Choose a School > Students > Naviance Student Login to start your college and career planning. 

- Student-athletes: Attend NCAA information sessions and consider registering with the NCAA Eligibility Center. 



Bentonville Schools 

High School Course Catalog, January 2026 

93 

## **College Planning Activities for Sophomores** 

###### **FALL Semester** 

- **Remember:** Selective colleges value strong grades in challenging courses more than perfect grades in easier ones. Take core subjects all four years, and plan for 2–3 years of a world language if you’re aiming for a selective school. 

- Get involved in clubs and student organizations. 

- If pursuing the IB Diploma Programme, enroll in at least Spanish II, French II, or Chinese II (assuming Year I is complete). Official IB Diploma enrollment takes place this spring during the SSP scheduling process. 

- Student-athletes: Attend NCAA information sessions and register with the NCAA Eligibility Center. 

- Participate in a PSAT/NMSQT preparation program. All sophomores will take the PSAT. Be sure to check the box on the test form to opt in to receive emails from colleges and universities. 

###### **SPRING Semester** 

- Review your PSAT score report. Compare missed items with correct answers to strengthen future test performance. 

- Meet with your counselor to plan junior-year courses - include as many academic and advanced options as you can while maintaining success. Explore dual enrollment opportunities for college credit. 

###### **Throughout the Year** 

- Maintain strong grades. 

- Gather and review information about different colleges and universities. 

- Investigate the costs of college programs to begin financial planning. 

- **Make college visits.** As a sophomore, you may take one excused day for a college visit. You can also take advantage of summer, holiday breaks, and family vacations to explore campuses. 

- Access your Naviance Student account (Bentonvillek12.org > Schools > Choose a School > Students > Naviance Student Login) to continue exploring college and career options. 

###### **Life Skills to Practice Being an Adult** 

As you move from sophomore to junior year, it’s also time to practice important life skills that will help you thrive in college and adulthood: 

- **Create a dedicated email account for college.** You’ll be surprised by how many messages you receive during the admissions process. 

- **Manage your email.** When you open a message, get in the habit of responding, deleting, or saving it. 

- **Get a job.** Not only does work experience impress admissions officers, but it also helps you mature and build responsibility. 

- **Learn to be a good future roommate.** Practice skills like making your bed, doing your own laundry, cleaning the bathroom, and tidying up after yourself. 

- **Budget your money.** Learn to make wise spending decisions and save when possible. 

- **Think carefully about social media.** Be mindful of what you post and how it reflects on you. 

- **Develop time-management strategies.** Find the tools and habits that help you stay organized and maintain a healthy balance. 

**Identify a trusted adult.** Have someone you can always go to for guidance and support. 



Bentonville Schools 

High School Course Catalog, January 2026 

94 

## **College Planning Activities for Juniors** 

###### **Summer (Before Junior Year)** 

- **Remember:** Selective colleges value strong grades in challenging courses. Continue with core subjects all four years, and plan for 2–3 years of a world language. 

- Student-athletes: Register with the NCAA Eligibility Center at ncaa.org if you plan to play college-level sports. 

###### **Fall Semester** 

- Focus on junior-year grades—take as many academic and advanced courses as possible, and explore dual enrollment opportunities. 

- Participate in community service projects. 

- Take the PSAT/NMSQT (check the box for college mailing lists) and consider registering for the ACT/SAT. 

- Meet with your counselor to plan senior-year courses - include as many academic and advanced options as possible. Explore dual enrollment opportunities for college credit. 

- Research and contact colleges and universities that interest you. 

- Review scholarship and college information.  Here 

###### **SPRING Semester** 

- Begin applications for ROTC scholarships or U.S. Service Academies if interested. 

- Research upcoming college admission essay prompts (see the Common Application site) and begin drafting. 

- **Make college visits.** As a junior, you may take three excused day for a college visit. You can also take advantage of summer, holiday breaks, and family vacations to explore campuses. 

- Meet with college reps who visit your high school. 

- Participate in SAT/ACT prep programs. 

- Take the SAT or ACT. All juniors will take the state-administered ACT. 

###### **Summer (Prior to Senior Year)** 

- Narrow your college list to 4–6 schools (include a “sure bet,” a few “good prospects,” and one “dream school”). 

- Keep notes on the pros and cons of each college. 

- Gather admissions and financial aid information. 

- Continue refining your personal statements/essays—highlight unique experiences and tell your story. 



Bentonville Schools 

High School Course Catalog, January 2026 

95 

## **College Planning Activities for Seniors** 

Note: SAT/ACT dates are listed throughout the year, but choose the testing dates that best fit your needs. 

###### **August / September** 

- **Remember:** Selective colleges value strong grades in challenging courses. Keep core subjects all four years, and plan for 2–3 years of a world language. 

- Log in to Naviance Student and update your “Colleges I’m Applying To” list. 

- Check your credits to confirm graduation and college admission requirements. 

- Register for and take college admission tests if needed. 

- Finalize a list of 4–6 colleges (include a definite acceptance, solid options, and a dream school). 

- Begin the application process—many schools use the Common Application. 

- Attend the Senior Class meeting with the College & Career Counselor 

- Start applying for scholarships and grants (see Naviance). 

- Request recommendations early and provide a resume. 

- Schedule college visits and meet with college reps. 

- Ask your parents to gather financial info for upcoming aid applications. 

###### **October / November / December** 

- Apply! Allow plenty of time for essays and responses. 

- Meet early action/early decision deadlines (as early as October or November). 

- Take/retake the SAT/ACT if necessary. 

- Complete FAFSA with your parents (opens Oct. 1). 

- Apply for Arkansas Department of Higher Education scholarships (opens Oct. 1). 

- Stay focused—first semester grades matter. 

- Research academic departments at top-choice schools. Ask current students about programs. 

- Complete college applications and request transcripts through Naviance. 

- Review application timeline and ensure all steps are complete. 

- Send official SAT/ACT scores to colleges. 

###### **February** 

- Keep grades up! 

- Check deadlines for scholarships, grants, and financial aid. 

- Look for details on Bentonville Community Scholarships. 

###### **March** 

- Deadline: Apply for Bentonville Community Scholarships. 

###### **April** 

- Review admissions decisions and financial aid letters. 

- Make final choice by May 1. Notify the school you will attend and decline others. 

- If wait-listed, notify the college if you wish to remain under consideration. 

- Finalize housing, scholarships, and financial aid plans. 

###### **May** 

- May 1 = College Decision Day! 

- Record your final choice and scholarships in Naviance. 

- Request a final transcript be sent via Naviance. 

- Take AP Exams if enrolled.

## **Scholarships & Financial Aid** 

With the costs of higher education continuously increasing, most students will need to consider seeking scholarships or some other form of financial aid. The choices made when entering high school can affect a student’s likelihood of gaining assistance. To have the highest probability for gaining the financial assistance, you should: 

- Select and pursue a rigorous program of courses that will well prepare you for seeking a degree/ area of interest to you. 

- Make good grades and maintain a good attendance record. 

- Participate in extracurricular and volunteer activities. 

- Take the appropriate college entrance exam(s). 

- Interview someone in your field of interest. 

- Visit the College & Career Center to access post-secondary opportunities. 

- Visit Arkansas Academic Challenge Scholarship: <u>sams.adhe.edu/Scholarship/Details/ACST</u> 

- Visit Arkansas Future Grant: <u>sams.adhe.edu/Scholarship/Details/ARFUTURE</u> 

- Visit Governor’s Distinguished Scholarship: <u>sams.adhe.edu/Scholarship/Details/GS</u> 

By following these guidelines, you increase your possibility of gaining some type of financial aid. There are generally four major types of financial aid available to students. They are as follows: 

- Scholarships-awards based on merit (either academic or some area of talent); 

- Grants-awards based on financial need which do not need to be repaid; 

- Loans-funds which are loaned through a bank, a college, or a lending institution with interest rates; and, 

- Work-Study Programs-jobs that allow students to earn money toward their education and which are coordinated through the college’s financial aid office. 

###### **<u>United States Service Academies</u>** 

- US Military Academy at West Point: <u>westpoint.edu</u> 

- US Merchant Marine Academy: <u>usmma.edu</u> 

- US Coast Guard Academy: <u>uscga.edu</u> 

- US Naval Academy: <u>usna.edu</u> 

- US Air Force Academy: <u>usafa.af.mil</u> 

###### **<u>U.S. Military and U.S. Armed Forces Programs</u>** 

The US military has five branches of service. The Air Force, Army, Marine Corps, and Navy fall under the Department of Defense. The Coast Guard falls under the Department of Homeland Security. Each service has a unique mission, which is reflected in its training, equipment, locations, and military culture. To determine which branch of the military might interest you go to usa.gov > Military and veterans > <u>Requirements to join the U.S. military.</u> 

- Army: <u>goarmy.com</u> 

- Navy: <u>navy.com</u> 

- Air Force: <u>airforce.com</u> 

- Marines: marines.com 

- Coast Guard: gocoastguard.com 

- Air National Guard: <u>ang.af.mil</u> 

- Army National Guard: <u>nationalguard.com</u> 

- Army Reserve: <u>goarmyreserve.com</u> or <u>army.mil/usar</u> 

- Air Force Reserve: afreserve.com 

- Marine Forces Reserve: <u>marforres.marines.mil</u> 

- Naval Reserve Force: <u>navyreserve.navy.mil</u> 

- Coast Guard Reserve: gocoastguard.com/reserve-careers
